ParlaMint-PT_2023-09-22.u4	Mr. President, ladies and gentlemen. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. Ministers and Secretaries of State: Once again, with a worrying recurrence of lack of measures to defend public school, the school year began with a severe lack of teachers — 92 000 students of basic and secondary education are without all teachers. The situation has worsened, wounding the essential core of guarantees of a quality public school that promotes equity and the development of children and young people. At the beginning of this week, the PCP members were on the ground, in contact with leaders of schools, teachers and other educational workers, students and education officers, confirming a picture of degradation and frustration that needs to be stopped. Since the lack of teachers and non-teaching staff, i.e. technicians and assistants of educational action, for example, problems in canteens and lack of works, going through the precariousness of professionals and the serious effects of mobility, the picture replicates from north to south of the country, with special incidence in the south. In the Faro district, the scenario is serious, with 8800 students without teachers at all disciplines; in the Beja district there were 4130 students; in Lisbon, in the school Queen Dona Amélia, for example, at least one class of 7. the year did not have seven teachers; in the school Maria Pia there are 16 teachers. Finally, we could give other examples. The situation is not of today, it has taken decades and is responsible, the successive PSD or PSD/CDS governments, as well as causes, such as disinvestment in teacher training, devaluation of teaching career, precariousness and a model of itinerance, which forces thousands of professionals to move every year to hundreds of miles away without any support. That is how thousands of young people prepared for teaching, including their professionalization, ended up not entering a career that did not match their fair expectations, and that is how we reached the point of rupture, with the impossibility, at least, to compensate the graduates for retirement. Between January and September, 2200 teachers retired, estimating that the volume of exits until the end of December rose to 3,500, which means that more students will be left without a teacher, while only a little more than 1 thousand newly trained in higher education institutions arrived at schools. Emergency measures are needed to provide the public school with teachers, technicians and other appropriate professionals to ensure the quality and equity of education in public school. The fight against precariousness and the offer of attractive and future careers, from the point of view of wages and progressions, are unavoidable imperatives. The end of precariousness and the linkage of all teachers with three or more years of service is required. The recognition of service time, the elimination of progression quotas between steps, the fair remuneration and progression of teachers and other professionals, from psychologists to operational assistants, passing through social service technicians and therapists. These measures, moreover, reflected in the PCP draft resolution on the valorization of education workers and public school, are also indispensable for the recovery of learning, hampered by the lack of time credits to schools, lack of funding and lack of teachers. The end of the re-use of school textbooks 1 is also indispensable. the cycle, which the government has just announced, giving reason to the CFP, but to which it must add the effective freeness of the paperbooks, which continue to burden families. With regard to higher education, there is a strong shortage of 104 000 beds in public student homes. The government is well aware of a residence building programme, which does not leave the paper, but it intends to move from only 15,073 beds in 2021, to only 26,772 in 2026, which corresponds only to 22,5 % of the 119 000 students displaced. The urgent expansion of the public housing network, the increase in the value of complement housing, among other measures, is required. It is also the case of canteens whose insufficiency is manifest and in which prices are being exacerbated. It is also urgent to combat the precariousness of higher education teachers, which reaches 53% — I repeat, 53%! — the total number of teachers as well as researchers, which is 75 % or even 80 %, when we consider the scholarships in the projects and research units. Mr. President, ladies and gentlemen. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. Ministers and State Secretaries, this situation cannot continue. It is imperative, in fact, to value public school. Reduction of the distances from the pedagogical zone tables from 10 to 63 ; 4. Annual recovery of vacancies from 2024, which will improve the chances of approaching residence ; 5. Opening of more than 20 000 school board places in the 2024 competition ; 6. Regularization of the professional situation of technological education teachers who did not have access to the competition of teachers; 7. ParlaMint-PT_2023-09-22.u328	Mr. President, ladies and gentlemen. Members: The bill submitted to the Assembly of the Republic intends to authorize the Government to create the database of inhibitions and dismissals, thus ensuring full transposition of Directive ( EU ) 2019 / 1151, which has amended Directive ( EU ) 2017 / 1132 with regard to the use of digital tools and procedures in the field of corporate law. Many of the measures encouraged by the Directive are widely known by Portuguese companies and entrepreneurs and are available online in the registration services of the Digital Justice Platform. In fact, in the past six years, justica.gov.pt reported with 185 services, more than 20 million visitors and about 50 million shares carried out only in the last year. Portugal anticipated 15 years to dematerialize the business creation process emanating from the same Directive ( EU ) 2017 / 1132. It is important to remember that, among us, it is possible to create companies online since 2006. Currently, two in three companies are made up of the Internet, and more than 244 000 companies have already been created, since this service started completely online. In June this year, after a national public hearing programme, we took an important step towards making this procedure simpler, more efficient and integrated. We launched the Online 2.0 Company in May of this year, which made it possible to build a society in just 10 minutes, being possible to electronically sign social pacts, make payments in different modalities or even create anonymous companies in a more efficient and faster system, with pre-filled data that the Public Administration already has, always with its own permission, exempting it from providing more data to the Administration. The company's life cycle is, in fact, one of the most fundamental reforms included in the Recovery and Resilience Plan for Justice and aims to give integrated support to companies, from its creation to eventual extinction. By the end of the first quarter of 2024, the platform will provide new features, which will, for example, allow the creation of fully online branches, still this year, or the Central Registry of Effective Beneficiary. In order to achieve, among other things, this relevant goal, the Institute of Registers and the Notary(IRN) has ongoing investments of more than EUR 30 million, of a total of 40 million provided for in the RPP, representing 71 % of its RPP already contracted or in proceedings on the street. This modernisation movement adds to the multi-annual commitment to hiring new workers, which is already under way. Ladies and gentlemen. Members, with the creation of this database, to be integrated into the business life cycle and into the Online Enterprise 2.0 platform, it will be possible to ensure more rigorous compliance with the measures of inhibition and dismissal decreed by the courts and regulatory entities, both in Portugal and in the other countries of the European internal market. First of all, as soon as these authorities communicate the decision of inhibition and dismissal to the IRN, it shall be recalled to the commercial register, informal and free of charge, the termination of the functions of managers, administrators, directors, representatives and liquidators. On the other hand, where, at the time of the request for registration of the appointment or retraining of members of social bodies, the commercial registration services shall, by consultation with the database of inhibitions and dismissals, verify that the person concerned is prevented from carrying out his or her duties, the request for commercial registration shall be refused. And interoperability mechanisms are also created between the database and the European system of commercial registration interconnection, BRIS ( Business Registers Interconnection System ), which will enable commercial registration services: communicate whether a particular natural person is inhibited, on the basis of the information contained in the database; or also request information from the various Member States on inhibitions and removals available in their national registers. This interoperability requires technological developments that will be achieved by the end of 2024, which means that in the first phase communications will take place even more traditional methods, but will happen. Ladies and gentlemen. Members, with the introduction by the justice of these communication and control mechanisms, it is intended not only to comply with the European directive, but above all greater transparency, more confidence and consequently greater competitiveness of the national and European economy. [[P.S.'s applause.]] [[Meanwhile, he took the presidency of Vice President Editi Estrela.]]

ParlaMint-PT_2023-09-22.u330	Madam President, Mrs. Minister, Mrs. State Secretaries, Mrs and Mrss. Members: This is a government claim in which the PSD sees itself. In fact, the creation of a database of inhibitions and dismissals is relevant, in particular in situations where, for the exercise of trade, for the occupation of certain positions or even for the administration of foreign heritage, as is the case with the curator, people are dismissed judicially or inhibited, so that this can be public and known, providing security and greater confidence in trade relations between people. Therefore, the Directive provides for a mechanism for exchanging information on whether a particular person is prevented from exercising the position of administrator of a company as well as other relevant information, and allows Member States to refuse the appointment of an administrator subject to an inhibition of the exercise of that position in another State. It is no doubt appropriate that such abusive behaviour as members of social bodies of commercial companies can be verified, thus ensuring that the competent authorities of the Member States can verify whether the person is not prohibited from exercising as an administrator, which appears to be quite important for the safety and confidence of the various economic operators. To this end, the competent authorities should also know whether the person concerned is registered in any of the relevant inhibition records in other Member States, so we also believe that the system of interconnection of company records between the various Member States is important. Therefore, what is intended is to create a database, which will remain under the scope of the Institute of Registers and the Notary and will, of course, entail changes to the Commercial Register Code, among others. So far, it's all right. However, this authorization, as well as the content of the authorized decree, leaves a few loose ends, some doubts, which I will state here, only in the form of a warning, and, if they understand relevant, will always go in time to make the proper reflection on these issues. First, the clear and effective regulation of who are the entities that can consult this database. After the opinions that were together on the request, it is important to consider whether only those listed on the list are sufficient. There won't be others eventually? Titleing entities, which grant titles, make contracts, make writings? It is not only the registers and notariates and the conservatives who, at this time in Portugal, make public writings and title, give titles to others. Therefore, it is good to reflect on this and to see if they are all there or if, eventually, they may still be missing one or the other. These digital tools and procedures that you have spoken about, which are essential, indeed, to this evolution, to this control, to this confidence and that are very important, can never cease to be harmonized with guaranteeing, robust, anti-fraud and abuse mechanisms, ensuring the protection of the people concerned. And I think you'll also have to guard this with some security. As for the organization of the database, it was also open. There are three models: centralized, decentralized and distributed, functionally or geographically. We did not realize what was the choice, and it would be important to realize which technical choice will be made for the database model, so that we can also have a perception about whether it will be the most appropriate for our specific case. in this same sense, some of the recommendations made in the opinions, in particular the National Data Protection Commission ( CNPD ). I cannot finish without giving the most important note, in my opinion. The PSD considers it useful, as I have already said, to create this database, which is relevant and absolutely effective, but leaves this warning: if, in inhibitions, its duration is associated with the decision it is intended to make - therefore, inhibition will always be for a certain time, the judicial decision itself clearly says «is inhibited for x years», that is clear – is no longer so as to dismiss. Many dismissals have no temporary limitation, so you do not realize how long the person will stay in the database: if it is the time of Article 7. the, which is not more than 20 years old, or if it is another that eventually understands more reasonable. It is that, attention, 20 years, for a person who is dismissed at the age of 30 or 40, is a sort of perpetual penalty, and life sentence is prohibited in Portugal. This assessment of proportionality may be barred in some constitutional constraints, so I leave this alert here. [[PSD applause.]]

ParlaMint-PT_2023-09-22.u333	For an intervention, Mrs Alma Rivera of the CFP Parliamentary Group has the floor.
ParlaMint-PT_2023-09-22.u334	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen. Members of the Government, Mrs. Members: The directive, as has already been mentioned and as the explanatory statement itself, provides for such mechanisms to exchange information on whether a particular person is prevented from exercising the position of an administrator of a company, or other relevant information, and allows Member States to refuse the appointment of a person as an administrator if it is subject to an inhibition of the exercise of managerial positions in another State. Thus, it is intended to protect all people who interact with societies or branches in the face of possibly fraudulent or abusive behavior. Therefore, the Government is asking for this authorisation because it is necessary to create a database of inhibitions and dismissals, in which information on these situations will be organized, and also seeking to prevent this type of behaviour that we have referred to earlier. These are worthy objectives, of course, and which we can not fail to follow, but which end up relating to aspects that deserve increased care, taking into account the protection of personal data and sensitive data and even the very compatibility of what is provided for in the directive and then in what the Government requests authorization to enshrine, with the GDPR itself (General Data Protection Regulation). There are aspects which have already been mentioned here and which have to do with the differentiation that must be made between inhibitions and dismissals and these aspects are all referred to by the National Data Protection Commission, which in some cases even advances concrete solutions, which I think we should consider and analyze and, eventually, to receive or seek other contributions that may be useful to the specialized work. We are, of course, available for this proposal to be as cautious as possible, ensuring this increased protection of Member States and citizens against criminal activities. [[PCP applause.]]

In this way, it is intended to achieve objectives of legislative harmonisation at European level, obtaining greater legal certainty, which will be an engine for facilitating the cross-border commercial establishment, in turn, of promoting the economy, employment and wealth creation, without, of course, neglecting the most vulnerable, workers and minority partners who can disagree with the project for what are created frameworks for the protection and promotion of rights that are covered by the draft decree-law that is intended to be authorised. The Directive also lays down an obligation relating to the prior supervision of the legality of the above-mentioned cross-border operations and, for this purpose, it has been defined, as an entity that monitors the legality of these procedures, as commercial registration services. The internal legal framework already provides for a set of rules relating to cross-border mergers, and it is therefore necessary to establish the legal framework to support other cross-border societal changes. A set of procedures is established that it is important to respect the preparation of a project on cross-border merger, division or transformation, preparation of reports on the operation...

ParlaMint-PT_2023-09-22.u384	For an intervention, Mr Jorge Mendes of the PSD Parliamentary Group has the floor.
ParlaMint-PT_2023-09-22.u385	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen. Members: The Proposal for a Law No 90 / XV / 1. a ( GOV ) is intended to authorize the Government to lay down specific rules on cross-border transformations, mergers and divisions by transposing to the internal legal order of Directive ( EU ) 2019 / 2121 of the European Parliament and of the Council. This legislative authority, submitted by the Government, in accordance with the Constitution and the Rules of Procedure of the Assembly of the Republic, promotes amendments to the Code of Commercial Societies, the Code of Commercial Register and the Decree-Law No 24 / 2019, which lays down the rules applicable to electronic communication between the national commercial register and the commercial registers of other Member States. The merger of companies is due, as is the legal practice, to a strict procedural ritual, imperative in the case of cross-border mergers, in accordance with the rules on internal mergers, in particular those relating to the process of decision-making on mergers, the protection of creditors of merging companies, bondholders and workers' rights. In order to achieve a cross-border merger, one of the participating companies must be based in a cross-border region, these unique regions of Portugal and the European Union, and the other shall be constituted in accordance with the legislation of one Member State, with the headquarters, central administration or principal establishment in its territory. In view of the explanation of the reasons for this initiative of the Government, the concern is to remove restrictions on freedom of establishment by promoting cross-border trade, competition and the protection of other actors through the implementation, inter alia, of previous, repeated monitoring instruments prior to the production of the effects of such operations. Consequently, in the area of supervision and in order to monitor the records of such companies, there is the crossing of relevant information between the national trade registers and those of the various Member States of the European Union involved in cross-border operations. In accordance with the opinion of the National Data Protection Commission, the Commission concludes that the project under review does not raise any reservations or observations as to its compliance with the national data protection regime. Similarly, the Assembly of the Republic, in the course of the legislative process, consulted the Governments of the Autonomous Regions of the Azores and Madeira, to which 15 other public, business and trade union entities, whose contributions in general do not call into question the transposition of the Directive, despite some reservations and suggestions sent to this Assembly, in particular the Confederation of Portuguese Industry, the Confederation of Trade and Services of Portugal, the General Union of Workers, the General Confederation of Portuguese Workers — National Trade Union, the Superior Council of the Magistracy, the Institute of Registers and the Notary and the Order of Officials and Executive Officers. Ladies and gentlemen. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. Ministers, ladies and gentlemen. The PSD will monitor the initiative under review and will vote-CL, in general, on the expectation that the Government will assume, in its office, by appropriate proposals and suggestions contained in the opinions of consulted entities and that they are part of the legislative process. [[PSD applause.]]

ParlaMint-PT_2023-09-22.u387	Madam President: The transposition of the directive we have discussed recently introduced a new instrument that focused more on individuals; this is a deepening of something the European Union has done practically since its inception, which is to enable companies to have cross-border activities, to move from one country to another and to carry out mergers and, if mergers, also necessarily cross-border divisions. This is a proposal that also includes some instruments for digital updating, which was already possible for companies, and makes things easier for small businesses. This directive has had some amendments from the European Parliament which have been very positive in terms of the involvement of workers in these processes — and could have gone further, of course — but it seems to me that this is another step forward in terms of the internal market, which alone is positive and which we can follow. However, this does not mean that, from the point of view of the national governments, there is no need to be very careful later so that these possibilities of greater mobility that companies now have do not harm the Portuguese economy. That is to say, we want these instruments to be used in favour of strengthening our economy, rising value scale, in terms of more companies incorporating knowledge and technology, but we do not want this to facilitate the escape of companies that are interesting to the Portuguese economy. This is another part of history, it has nothing to do with the vote in this directive, it has to do with all government action.
ParlaMint-PT_2023-09-22.u388	For an intervention, Mrs Inês de Sousa Real has the floor of the PAN.
ParlaMint-PT_2023-09-22.u389	Madam President, ladies and gentlemen. Members of the Government here present, ladies and gentlemen. Members: Although we agree with the objectives of this initiative, since we believe that they can enable us to identify abusive or fraudulent purposes, which lead or aim to lead to fraud or avoidance of European law or national law, in any case, we cannot fail to mention two concerns. On the one hand, we are concerned about the training and bureaucracy associated with the procedure so that this control of legality does not go beyond formality, which is essential for the services to which supervision is restricted. It is therefore important to see how it will materialise, so that, in particular, at the level of the services of the commercial register, they have adequate internal training and procedure and that the transposition of the directive is not dead letter. The Institute of Registers and notaries itself has already requested mechanisms to assist them in their assessment of these requests and in their safe decision-making, as well as the possibility of recourse to an independent expert. They are therefore the services themselves that ask for help in the implementation of the law and the minimum requirement here is that these mechanisms be given. On the other hand, we are not sure about the protection of workers in this scheme, specifically as regards the right to information and participation. And here we understand that transposition falls short of what is intended and we are concerned about how the clarification of the information itself and the formation of its representatives in this same process will be ensured, which questions we would like to see answered here today.
ParlaMint-PT_2023-09-22.u390	For an intervention, Mr Jorge Botelho of the PS Parliamentary Group has the floor.
ParlaMint-PT_2023-09-22.u391	Madam President, Mrs. Minister, Mrs. Secretary of State, ladies and gentlemen. Members: As has already been said, with this bill the Government intends to obtain a legislative authorisation for the transposition of Directive ( EU ) 2019 / 2121 on cross-border transformations, mergers and divisions, harmonizing our internal legislation with European legislation. It is also intended to amend the regulations on registers and notaries, the Commercial Register Code and the Commercial Society Code so that they can be harmonised. In good time, the government does so, and we have already legislated on this matter in 2017, with the transposition of a 2017 directive, and now this whole regime deepens. For this reason Portugal has one of the lowest unemployment rates in Europe, or one of the highest employability rates in Europe. The Portuguese economy is competitive, open to competition and is, fortunately, attracting a large number of external investments, so that we also need to attract this regime from commercial companies, mergers, transformations and cross-border divisions. This legal regime adds to the legal regime we already have, because the existing legal regime adds a set of requirements. What do you intend to achieve? The full exercise with the abolition of restrictions on the freedom of establishment of commercial companies between Member States, as set out in Articles 49. o is 54. the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union. It is also intended to provide guarantees of adequate participation and protection to workers and creditors, through consultation of the proceedings, access to opinion and right of reply by the company concerned, and also to minority partners, by access to the court within the time limit for the exercise of its right, in addition to an effort to control legality, as has been mentioned here too, and prior monitoring of the transformations, mergers and cross-border divisions through the services, precisely registration and notariary. Finally, it is intended that the national trade registers of the Member States of the European Union, involved in cross-border operations, should contain the necessary information from the commercial registers of other Member States, in order to be able to follow the history of these companies. This is a set of possible transformations, as the Secretary of State said here and we are accompanying, in order to promote the economic climate, the opening up of the economic climate, the attraction of investment and the creation of enterprises. Therefore, given the development of Portuguese legislation, with clear benefits for economic activity, harmonisation in the applicable European legislative framework and guarantees given to workers' participation, procedural and record clarity, we will vote in favour of this proposal. [[P.S.'s applause.]]

Theodora Cardoso added to the high analytical rigor, to the enormous wealth of intellect and to the recognized use of vision, courage, independence of spirit and unconditional fidelity to the common good and public service. In the rare combination of all these qualities, his voice has been unmistakable in recent decades, contributing several times to alert public opinion to dangerous derivatives, not always successfully, not always of the pleasure of the powers instituted. He never gave concessions to anything, nor did anyone, often ran the risks associated with heterodoxy, was uncomfortable when he thought he could not quit being, keeping intact his commitment to what he always thought was the country's greatest interest. For all this, there is a huge debt of gratitude, which Theodora Cardoso's death prevents us to pay. Eduardo Pitta made homosexual identity matter of literary creation, but without abdicating the primacy of artistic quality. His style of writing sometimes violent and cutting, with a fine irony, an aggressive view of existence, an autobiographical path, left an original brand in national literary production. Article 7. o - Application requirements for the acquisition of the area right of the returned public real estate 1 — The application referred to in paragraph 4 of the preceding article must contain the offer of acquisition of the area right of the returned public real estate, indicating: (a) The identification of the tenderer, containing: (i) Name; 6 — For the purposes of point (d) of paragraph 1 ‘reconversion or rehabilitation’ means the procedures and undertakings deemed necessary for the buildings to be classified as intended for housing, including partial or total construction or reconstruction of the building. Article 7. o-B Form and application periods for the acquisition of the area right of the returned public real estate 1 — The application referred to in Article 7 ( 4 ). it is carried out electronically on the website to be made available by IHRU at http://www.portalda habitatacao.pt. 2 — For a period of three months after the first application, other interested parties may apply in accordance with the preceding article and propose tenders for the same building. 3 — In the course of that period, the IHRU shall arrange the tenders for their value by using the criteria of discharge for tenders of the same value: (a) first of all, the quantity of dwellings resulting from conversion or rehabilitation; (b) secondly, the antique of the offer, preferring the one presented in the first place. 4 — Upon expiry of the period laid down in paragraph 2, the IHRU shall notify the candidate who has delisted the application procedure in order to give the opportunity to equal the best bid placed on the date, available within one month to carry out an equivalent proposal. 5 — For the purposes of the above paragraphs, applicants may request support from IHRU or other bodies, including the regional or local public administration, which with those entities shall hold collaborative protocols in this specific area. 6 — All applications which are not rejected shall be made public on the same portal, providing all information concerning the applications to be completed and completed. 7 — The procedures relating to the application of the programme on the Internet, as well as the elements and documents necessary for the formalisation of applications in a form dematerialized by the candidates, are regulated at the door. Article 7. o-C Obligations 1 — Candidates, during the competitive procedure, after this procedure and as long as they have the right of surface, shall maintain all their information and the updated conversion or rehabilitation plan and shall notify the changes to IHRU within 10 days of their occurrence. 2 — The acquirer of the surface duty of the building shall, after payment of the supply value of the acquisition of the area duty, commence the procedures and undertakings necessary to complete the conversion or rehabilitation plan within the time limit laid down therein, considering possible extensions which may exist, as provided for in Article 6. o-E. 3 — After the completion of the conversion or rehabilitation plan, the acquirer is obliged to rent the housing available in the rental programme accessible for a minimum of 10 years, unless the building is intended for its own and permanent housing for an equal period of time. 4 — Acquisitioners of the area right shall be subject to verification by IHRU of the fulfilment of the conditions and duties to which they are bound for the acquisition of that right, in particular as regards the delivery of elements or documents and compliance with the conditions for the fulfilment of the conversion or rehabilitation plan. 5 — It is up to IHRU to carry out monitoring actions which it deems necessary to assess the fulfilment of obligations by applicants and acquirers of the area right, and to this end may request elements directly from those or use the procedure provided for in the following Article. Article 7. o-D Termination of the rights of the acquirer of surface duty 1 — The IHRU may terminate the area right of the building where the following situations occur: (a) Provision of false declarations by the purchasers; (b) Failure to comply with the conversion or rehabilitation plan within the time limit; (c) Failure to comply with the obligation to rent housing at affordable prices. 2 — The IHRU shall notify the acquirer of the draft decision to terminate the area duty in order to ensure that the latter proves that the facts are not verified. 3 — The failure to submit the evidence referred to in the previous paragraph within 30 working days from the notification of IHRU determines the termination of the building's surface duty, without prejudice to other legally applicable penalties. 4 — The termination of the surface duty in accordance with previous paragraphs does not confer on the purchaser the right to obtain any compensation. 5 — Where the area right ceases, the acquirer shall be prevented from applying for applications in other procedures for a period of two years and shall be subject to review all the procedures in progress in which the same is found. 6 — If the area duty ceases, the following candidates shall be notified in order of the tenders of the application which generated the procedure for the transmission of the area duty, allowing them to assume responsibility as acquirers, fulfilling the conversion or rehabilitation plan in force when the area right is lost or the conversion and rehabilitation plan is lost. 7 — In the absence of a candidate available, the building remains under IHRU, being available for new application procedure or management of the central, local or regional administration. Article 7. o-E Extension 1 — The acquirer of the surface duty may send to IHRU an application for an extension of the time limit provided for in Article 7(1)( d ) ( iii ). o -A. (b) other reasons provided that they are duly justified with documents from third parties, including situations of physical impossibility, natural disasters or other exceptional and subject to external validation. 3 — The IHRU may request the acquirer to submit documents supporting its application for extension. 4 — The IHRU shall notify the acquirer within 10 days of the draft decision and shall invite the acquirer to overcome the irregularities in the application. 5 — Applications for extension shall be rejected when the situations described in paragraph 2 are not found, or when the time limit for filling in irregularities or attaching documents, the acquirer shall not do so. Article 7. o-F Computer Platform 1 — The management of information on land and public buildings concession support is carried out through a computer platform created for this purpose, which includes a database. 2 — The purpose of the IT platform is to organize and keep up to date information on the applications referred to in Article 7. o -A. 3 — All entities to whom data processing falls under this Act shall perform such processing on this platform. Article 7. o-G Information security The IHRU is the entity responsible for the processing of the information contained in the information platform referred to in the preceding article, for the purpose of adopting the appropriate technical and organisational measures to protect data against destruction, accidental or unlawful, accidental loss, alteration, dissemination or unauthorized access, in accordance with the Personal Data Protection Act. Article 7. o-H Personal data 1 — The personal data referred to in Article 7 shall be collected for automated processing. o -A. 2 — The collection of the data referred to in the previous paragraph shall be carried out by filling in the electronic form existing on the computer platform, according to the model approved by the portal, in which applicants authorise the IHRU to confirm the data collected from the entities for such authorisations in accordance with the following Article. 3 — Applicants shall also authorise the publication of personal data provided for in Article 6 ( 1 ). o-B, except for sub-paragraphs ( ii ) and ( iv ) of point ( a ), on the Internet site to be made available by IHRU, at http://www.portalda habitatacao.pt. 4 — The lack of authorisation in accordance with previous paragraphs determines the rejection of the candidature. Article 7. o-I Data verification It is up to the IHRU to request by electronic means to the competent public services the verification of the personal data of candidates and acquirers relating to the real estate subject to acquisition of the area right, and those services shall refer the corresponding response to it in the same way within 10 days. Article 7. o-J Data conservation 1 — Personal data shall be kept for the period of time strictly necessary for the pursuit of the purpose for which it is intended, in accordance with Article 27. that of the Law on the Protection of Personal Data. 2 — The entities responsible for receiving and processing information shall be obliged to respect professional secrecy and prohibited from processing personal data without instructions from the responsible authority.

(b) the application of this reduction for a period between 6 and 12 months, renewable at the request of the borrower for a maximum period of 24 months; (c) the extension of the maturity of the credit for a period corresponding to the period for which the reduction of benefits is applied, even if it exceeds the limits set by the BdP for banking mutual contracts. 5 — The incomes relevant to the calculation of the rate of effort are those existing at the time of renegotiation of credit conditions and are determined by the average incomes obtained in the previous six months. 6 — The proof of income referred to in the previous paragraph may be made by the respective receipts of maturity or, where there is no such possibility, by other suitable documents, without the requirement for special formalities. 7 — The BP teams shall be responsible for verifying the conditions laid down in this Article. 8 — The deadline for the conclusion of the renegotiation shall be 30 days and the Government shall be responsible for the regulation of its procedure. 9 — The conditions resulting from the renegotiation of the credit shall apply to benefits over 30 days after the period provided for in the previous paragraph.

ANNEX[ Injurious] ( referred to in Article 22. ( o ) ANNEX 1 [ referred to in Article 44. o -A ] Creation of an exceptional scheme for the provision of devolved or underused buildings Chapter I General Part Article 1. the Purpose This Act regulates the programme of cooperation between the State and the Local Authorities for the use of inactive public real estate(Programa) and establishes a special procedure for the assignment of temporary use to municipalities, the parish with more than 10,000 inhabitants or inter-municipal entities of real estate in the public domain of the State and of immovable property in the private domain of the State and of public institutions which are devolved or underused. Article 2 the Objectives and content 1 — The Programme promotes collaboration between, on the one hand, state-owned public entities or managers of devolved or underused buildings and, on the other, municipalities, parishes with more than ten thousand inhabitants or intermunicipal entities (local public entities), with a view to the use and profitability of these buildings, the prevention of their degradation and the dynamization of the capillary management of the public heritage. 2 — Collaboration between public entities holding or managing real estate and local public entities may take place, inter alia, in the following ways: (a) Accomplishment of public buildings returned or underused in the territory of the local public entity; (b) Support of the local public entity in the administrative, registering or matricial regularisation of buildings; (c) Support of the local public entity in the process of alienation or costly transfer from the property to third parties; (d) Measures to preserve or rehabilitate buildings by the local public entity; (e) Temporary assignment of the property to the local public entity for the realization of projects of public interest. Article 3 the real estate covered 1 — This law covers the property of the public domain of the State and the property of the private domain of the State and of public institutions which are devolved or underused. 2 — For the purposes of this Act: (a) Returned property, any urban building or autonomous fraction that, having areas that can be used, is unoccupied; (b) Underused property, all urban building or autonomous fraction whose areas effectively used correspond to less than 1 / 4 of the useful areas available. 3 — For the purposes of this law, they relate to urban and mixed buildings. 4 — For the purposes of paragraph 2 ( a ), the absence of contracts in force with water, gas and electricity supply undertakings or the absence of invoicing relating to water, gas and electricity consumption shall be evidenced. Article 4 the subjective scope The Programme regulated by this Act covers the following: (a) Local public entities, which are municipalities, or parish with more than 10,000 inhabitants, or intermunicipal entities; (b) State public entities which are holders or managers of the buildings referred to in the preceding article, in particular bodies, services and entities of the direct and indirect administration of the State. Article 5. the principle of burden 1 — The forms of collaboration referred to in the article shall involve some form of counterpart, either through the collaboration provided by the local public body or through the transfer of temporary use of the property. (b) the provision of services or incorporation of goods with a view to the use, profitability, conservation or rehabilitation of buildings; Article 6. The monitoring of Programme 1 — The members of the Government responsible for the Treasury and Local Administration shall appoint a supervisor for the duration of the Programme. ( b) verify, by sampling and using the stock exchange of evaluators from the Directorate-General for Treasury and Finance ( DGTF ), the assessments of the buildings submitted by the tenderers as provided for in Chapter III ; 3 — The single prosecutor appointed from among the official auditors of accounts or companies of official auditors of accounts entered in their list of the Order of Official Accountants and the Securities Market Commission. Chapter II Cooperation for the return on assets Article 7. the articulation of cooperation by the Directorate-General for Treasury and Finance 1 — Local public entities may, on request or with the agreement of DGTF referred to in Article 8 ( 2 ) provide any of the services referred to in Article 2 ( a) and ( d ). o 2 — Any public entity holding property referred to in Article 3. it may request the cooperation of local public bodies under the Programme regulated by this Act and shall simultaneously inform DGTF of the request. Article 8. the lifting of devolved or under-used public buildings 1 — The local public entity may draw up and submit to DGTF a survey of the devolved or under-used public buildings located in its territory. 2 — The survey referred to in the previous paragraph shall be drawn up in a form made available by DGTF on its website. 3 — The local public authority which shall issue a public property collection valid and completed in accordance with the terms set out in the FRG form shall receive as a counterpart credits for temporary transfer of public property to be contracted under Chapter III, in the following proportion depending on the number of inhabitants: (a) ≤ 10 thousand inhabitants, the power to use over one property for every three buildings identified in the survey and validated by FRG ; (b) > 10 thousand and ≤ 50 thousand inhabitants, the power to use over one property for every five buildings identified in the survey and validated by FRG ; (c) > 50 thousand inhabitants, the power to use over one property for every ten buildings identified in the survey and validated by FRG. 4 — The power duty referred to in the preceding paragraph shall be increased by 100 % if the buildings identified in the survey are classified. Article 9. o Support in administrative regularisation, register! or real estate matrix; 1 — DGTF or the entity holding the property, with the knowledge of that property, may request support from the local public entity in the material and legal acts necessary for the regularisation of the property in terms of urban planning licensing, horizontal property constitution, registration in the residential register, matrix registration or batch operation. 2 — As opposed to the support service provided for in the previous paragraph, the local public entity may receive: (a) appropriations for the assignment of temporary use of public buildings to be contracted under Chapter III; (b) a cash payment in accordance with the emolumentary table, approved by office of the members of the Government responsible for the areas of treasure and local administration, within 90 days of the publication of this law and after the hearing of the National Association of Portuguese Municipalities and the National Association of Freguesias. 3 — The determination of the counterparty of the local public body shall be made in the order of DGTF referred to in Article 7 ( 3 ). the 4 — In the order referred to in the previous paragraph, DGTF may confer on the local public entity the powers of representation of the property holder for the practice of the acts necessary for administrative, register or matrix regularisation. Article 10. Support in the transfer or transfer process from property to third parties; 1 — DGTF or the entity holding the property, with the knowledge of that property, may request the local public authority: (a) operational support in the promotion of return or under-used public property profitability operations, including their placing on the market and identification of potential stakeholders, in strict compliance with applicable public procurement rules; (b) receiving the temporary leased property for sub-cession or lease to third parties. 2 — As opposed to the activities provided for in the previous paragraph, the local public body may receive any of the counterparties referred to in paragraph 2 of the preceding Article. Article 11. the maintenance and preservation of buildings by the Local Entity; 1 — The DGTF or the entity holding the property, with the knowledge of the property, may contract with the local public entity: (b) a percentage of the product to be received by the holder in the event of profitability of the property; 3 — In the case of rehabilitation operations carried out under this Act, the local public entity may promote and subscribe applications for allowances and support to which the project is eligible. Chapter III Special procedure for the assignment of temporary use Article 12. the Initiation of proceedings The procedure laid down in this chapter begins with the presentation of a proposal for a concession of use by the local public authority, which in this chapter is designated as « Proponent ». Article 13. The proposal for a concession of use 1. The proposer may submit to the DGTF a proposal for the assignment of the use of immovable property which is devolved or under-utilised, with a view to bringing it to an end corresponding to the tasks pursued by him. 2. The proposer is always transferable from the property and the first responsible for fulfilling the legal and contractual obligations relating to it. 3. The proposal to be submitted by the proposer shall include: (a) a project for the use of the property as provided for in the following Article; (b) a proposal for the duration of the assignment of use; (c) an assessment of the property in accordance with Article 15. o, d ) A counter-repretation proposal as provided for in Article 23. (e) the demonstration of the proposal’s financial capacity appropriate to the implementation of the project. Article 14. the project of use of the property 1 — The project of use of the property identifies the activity or end of public interest to which the Proponent intends to target the use of the property, paying attention to its location, the rules of the municipal instrument of management of the applicable territory and possible classification of the property. 2 — The project also includes a descriptive memory of any works of preservation or rehabilitation of the property and changes that the Proponent intends to carry out in the property. 3 — The proposer may involve public or private partners in the implementation of the project, and in this case shall include in the proposal the general terms of such cooperation. 4 — The agreement provided for in the previous paragraph does not confer any rights on the assignor to any partners and automatically expires with the termination of the assignment. 5 — The choice of a private partner by the Proponent respects the rules of public procurement. Article 15. the valuation of the property 1 — The proposal submitted by the Proponent is accompanied by an assessment of the property carried out by the evaluator expert registered at the CMM, at the expense of the Proponent. 2 — The Single Prosecutor provided for in Article 6. the verification, by sampling and using the scholarship of assessors of the DGTF, the evaluations presented by the Proponents. Article 16. the Instruction 1 — Within 15 days of receipt of the proposal to DGTF shall promote the hearing: (a) of the ministry which protects the entity or service to which the property is affected; (b) of the owner of the property; (c) of the entity responsible for the classification, in the case of the property is classified. 2 — The entities referred to in the previous paragraph shall deliver their opinion on the proposal within 45 days of the notification by DGTF. 3 — DGTF shall immediately forward to the Rapporteur a copy of the opinions provided for in this Article. 4 — DGTF must listen to the rapporteur before taking the final decision, informing him, in particular, on the likely meaning of this decision. 5 — In the light of the opinions or draft decisions referred to in the previous paragraphs, the Proposal may reformulate its proposal. Article 17. Decision 1 — DGTF shall decide to accept or reject the proposal within 90 days of its receipt. 2 — In the event of a reformulation of the proposal pursuant to paragraph 2 of the preceding Article, the FFD shall have a period of 45 days to decide, extending the time limit of the previous paragraph if necessary. 3 — The tacit deferral of the proposal is made if, at the end of the period laid down for the decision, the proposal is not given and notified to the Rapporteur. 4 — The maximum head of the public legal person owned by the property or, in the absence thereof, the member of the government responsible for the financial area shall deliver to the Proponent the property contained in the proposal submitted by it within 60 days of the express or tacit deferral of the proposal. 5 — In the event of non-compliance with the provisions of the previous paragraph, the urban policy instruments referred to in Article 54 shall apply. 2 et seq. of the legal regime of urban rehabilitation, approved by Decree-Law No 307 / 2009 of 23 October 2009. 6 — The DGTF may only reject the proposal with the following grounds: (a) the illegality of the purposes or activities of the proposed use project; (b) the illegality or manifest disappropriation to the public interest of the works for the preservation or rehabilitation of the property provided for in the project; (c) the lack of demonstration of the financial capacity for the implementation of the project and its sustainability; (d) the manifest injury to the public interest in the acceptance of the counter-responsibility proposed by the Proponent, weighted the probability of alternatives to the profitability of the property; (e) the owner of the property has an alternative project for the use of the property, which is shown to start within six months ; (f) the DGTF or the owner of the property have a firm offer for the alienation or costly assignment of that which is generating revenue above that would result from the acceptance of the proposal of the Proponent. 7 — If the basis for the rejection of the proposal is one of the provisions of points (e) and (f) of the previous paragraph and the alternative operation does not take place within six months of the decision of DGTF, the proposer shall have the right to request the re-evaluation of the proposal without the same grounds for rejection being invoked. 8 — the act of disregarding the proposal shall be made available to the member of the Government responsible for the treasury area. Article 18. the Legal Representative without prejudice to the hearing referred to in Article 16. the and of the possible rights to the repayment supported by the Proponent, DGTF acts as legal representative of the owner of the property in the decision-making acts of the proposal and others that are necessary in the management of the buildings. Article 19. Form 1 — The favourable, express or silent decision of DGTF is quite a title for the tenderer to assume the use of the property and initiate the execution of the project. 2 — If the order of the DGTF is subject to condition, the contract shall form with the acceptance expressed by the Proponent of the conditions laid down in it. Article 20. the Expiry for non-implementation of project 1 — If the Proponent does not initiate the execution of the project within six months of the order of DGTF, the temporary assignment shall expire. 2 — At the rapporteur's reasoned request, DGTF may once again extend the period laid down in the previous paragraph. Article 21 the constitution of duties or charges The proposer may not constitute on the property burden or charges, except by prior and express authorization from DGTF. Article 22. the works of preservation and rehabilitation of the property The acceptance by DGTF of the proposal implies the authorization of the Proponent to carry out the works of conservation, rehabilitation or modification of the property that consists of descriptive memory integrated into the project. Article 23. the Financial Scheme of Allocation 1 — The temporary transfer of the property is in accordance with the principle of the burden enshrined in Article 54. odo Decree-Law No 280 / 2007 of 7 August, considering the assessment provided for in Article 15. (b) payment of income; c) delivery to the State of part of the revenue obtained from the operation of the property. 3 — In the event that the transfer is made solely by making investment in the property, the period of assignment shall be fixed according to the relationship between the current value of the property provided for in the assessment referred to in Article 15. the and the volume of investment in work to be carried out, plus a maximum period of three years to carry out the work. (b) 70 % for the owner of the property after the value of the investment is amortised. Article 24. the term of assignment of use 1 — The transfer of property shall be the maximum duration of 50 years. 2 — The DGTF may determine a period of less than the duration of the assignment proposed by the Proponent, on the basis of a significant drawback to the public interest of the duration proposed by the Proponent and in the extent to which the investment made by the Proponent is due. 3 — The advance resolution of the concession by DGTF implies communication to the Rapporteur in advance of at least 60 days per year missing for the end of the contract and compensation for the investment made and not yet amortised. Chapter IV Final provisions Article 25. This Act shall enter into force on the first day of the second month following its publication. Article 26. the Subsidiary Regime In everything that is not specifically regulated in this Act, the provisions of the public real estate scheme, approved by Decree-Law No 280/2007 of 7 August 2007 shall apply in its current wording.
